[9205] TOOLRESULT: 
- Several flags mean "skip": `-M` skips the ESM→PSM→ASM→RDBMS→keycloak transformation (NOT "model only"). `-B` skip backend, `-F` skip frontend, `-KA` skip Karaf, `-S` skip schema image.
- `-a` builds `app/` module only (fast Java iteration). `-f` frontend module only. `-sc` schema CLI JAR. `-d` / `--docker` Docker images. `-p` parallel. `-q` quick (cache, skip validations). `-i` ignore checksum errors and refresh checksums.
- Model-only changes need full `./judo.sh build` (no flags), not `-M`. `asm2keycloak` emits the Keycloak realm only when model build runs. With `-M` a stale realm sticks; app shows blank and `/system/health` lacks `JaxRsApplicationsReady`.

async function setSetting(admin: Admin, label: RegExp, value: string, kind: 'combo' | 'text') {
  const page = admin.page;
  await openSettings(page);
  if (kind === 'combo') {
    const box = page.getByRole('combobox', { name: label });
    if ((await box.inputValue()) !== value) {
      await box.click();
      await page.getByRole('option', { name: value, exact: true }).click();
      await expect(box).toHaveValue(value);
      await saveView(page);
    }
  } else {
    const box = field(page, label);
    if ((await box.inputValue()) !== value) {
      await typeInto(page, page, label, value);
      await page.screenshot({ path: '/tmp/before-save.png' });
      await saveView(page);
    }
  }
  await btn(page, /Back/).click();
}

/** Runs one job from the settings view (the "as of" moment stays empty = now); returns the message of the job. */

  const page = admin.page;
  await openSettings(page);
  await btn(page, button).click();

5e43f39 Round 17 (Level 9b): F76 carrier label + nightly sync (staging events), F77 mails from editable templates (outbox, Mailpit, retry/backoff), F78 cart reminder (sent marker), F79 5 000-row CSV/JSON import (runs, row errors, undo); admin cards Sent mails and Import runs, tracking on the customer shipment card; 21 ITs, level9b spec (7), GAPS R17-1..11, TRACE, UI-PLAN

[9297] ASSISTANT: F76–F79 are built and committed locally (`5e43f39`, screenshots in `a679fac`; nothing pushed). The `level9b` Playwright spec passes 7/7, the customer `--grep @smoke` run passes 19/19, and all 56 integration tests pass.

## Built
- **F76 carrier and nightly sync**
  - Shipping a sub-order asks the carrier for a label and stores the tracking number, label link and status. The carrier is a stub, set by the shop setting "Carrier": succeed, fail or timeout.
  - A carrier error gives a readable message and leaves the sub-order untouched.
  - The nightly sync saves each new status as a `CarrierEvent` first, then moves delivered parcels to DELIVERED. The same status is never saved twice.
  - The job runs at 02:00. The admin button "Sync carrier status" does the same work, with an optional "as of" time instead of waiting.
  - The customer's shipment card on the order shows tracking number and carrier status.
  - Order-level shipping (orders without seller sub-orders) still takes a hand-typed tracking number; the carrier covers sub-orders only.
- **F77 mails from templates**
  - The four mail templates (order confirmation, shipped, refund, cart reminder) are editable in the admin as "Mail templates".
  - Each mail is saved as an outbox row inside the business transaction, so a rolled-back change sends nothing.
  - A job sends the outbox every 5 seconds. Mailpit in Docker receives the mails.
  - A failing mail server retries after 1, 6, 21 and 51 minutes, then marks the mail FAILED. The admin button "Send queued mails" ignores the waiting time.
  - Editing a template changes the next mail only; earlier mails keep their old wording.
- **F78 cart reminder**
  - A cart untouched for 24 hours gets one reminder; the sent marker prevents a second.
  - Adding to the cart again starts a new quiet period.
  - Running the job twice sends one mail.
- **F79 import**
  - A CSV or JSON file of 5 000 rows becomes an import run that returns at once.
  - Rows are saved in batches of 250. The result is 4 990 products created and 10 errors listed with their data-row numbers.
  - Duplicate SKUs, in the file or already in the shop, are row errors. A file with the wrong header is refused.
  - "Undo import" removes the run's products that were never sold.
  - The screens stay usable while it runs. The 5 000-row import takes about 2.5 minutes.

## Screens
- **Admin cards:** "Sent mails" and "Import runs" (progress bar, created and error counts, Undo). Carrier events and Mail templates stay generated tables.
- **Customer:** the shipment card shows tracking and carrier status, labelled in Hungarian as well.
- Added to `UI-PLAN.md`.

## Intent

Keep every version of a product (or document) as a full, editable copy, grouped by a container entity, with a rule that only one version is approved at a time. This is a convention built on the platform; the platform offers no versioning feature, only the DAO calls the operations use.

## Practice

### Platform: the DAO calls the operations are built from

- Creation uses the generated `<Entity>ForCreate` builder and `dao.create`; relations to existing entities are set in the builder, and a single-relation setter needs a real object, never `null` ([builder pattern](../../agent-docs/backend/data-access-guide.md#which-types-get-a-full-dao), [[builder-pattern-entity-creation]]).
- Multi-valued relations are read with the DAO relation queries (`xDao

[9355] TOOLRESULT: 
Admins load or dump a whole set of records (catalog items, master data, reference lists) as one JSON file. The file goes through the file store; the import operation reads it, validates and deduplicates each record and creates entities, and the export operation serializes the entities back to a file.

## Practice

### Platform base

- A file is passed as a binary attribute: upload is two-phase and the operation input carries a file-store token; invalid tokens fail with `INVALID_FILE_TOKEN` ([binary upload tokens](../../agent-docs/backend/platform-behavior-reference.md#binary-upload-tokens)).
- The operation is a custom implementation (OSGi component) that injects DAOs and services with `@Reference` ([custom operations](../../agent-docs/backend/custom-operations.md#osgi-component-registration-pattern)). No interceptor protects it, so check the caller's role inside ([which operations are intercepted](../../agent-docs/backend/interceptors.md#which-operations-are-intercepted)).
- Duplicate checks use the fluent query with typed filters and `count()` ([query patterns](../../agent-docs/backend/data-access-guide.md#query-patterns)); records are created through the generated builder ([[builder-pattern-entity-creation]]).
- JSON parsing is not a platform feature of the operation: use Jackson in your own code. The platform's shared `ObjectMapper` for REST is configured separately ([custom REST endpoints and CXF](../../agent-docs/backend/platform-behavior-reference.md#custom-rest-endpo
[9356] TOOLRESULT: ## Intent

A parent entity owns a set of mutable child records (for example monthly plans). At a significant moment (approval, publication, every change) the owner needs to keep what the data looked like. Copy the children into a version entity with a timestamp and summary values, and never change that copy afterwards.

## Practice

### Copy in the same operation as the transition

The snapshot is plain custom code in a custom operation (or a service it calls). It uses generated DAOs only; the platform has no snapshot feature of its own.

1. Read the parent and its children through the relation query of the DAO. Pass a mask that lists only the fields you copy.
2. Create the version entity through the generated `ForCreate` builder, with the timestamp and the aggregated summary values.
3. Create one copy entity per child, linked to the version. The copies are separate entities with their own identifiers, not references to the live children.
4. Only then change the parent's state.

All of this runs in the transaction of the operation, so an exception in step 4 also rolls back the snapshot. Do not put the snapshot into an `async()` interceptor: it runs outside the transaction and loses its exceptions (see [Interceptors](../../agent-docs/backend/interceptors.md#the-interceptor-contract)).

### Make the history immutable by design

"Immutable" is a convention, not a platform guarantee. Model the version and copy entities without update or delete operations and do not expose them fo

export const SearchBox: FC = () => {
  const { t } = useTranslation();
  const { clearNavigate } = useJudoNavigation();
  const [value, setValue] = useState(readSearch());
  useEffect(() => {
    const sync = () => setValue(readSearch());
    window.addEventListener(SEARCH_CHANGED, sync);
    return () => window.removeEventListener(SEARCH_CHANGED, sync);
  }, []);
  const label = t('shop.search.placeholder', { defaultValue: 'Search products' }) as string;
  return (
    <InputBase
      value={value}
      onChange={(e) => setValue(e.target.value)}
      onKeyDown={(e) => {
        if (e.key === 'Enter') {
          writeSearch(value.trim());

  async list(
    owner?: JudoIdentifiable<any>,

    headers?: Record<string, string>,

              ...(headers ?? {}),

          ? { headers }
          : undefined,
    );

[9540] TOOLRESULT: ## Overview

`OperationCallInterceptor` is a powerful mechanism for implementing cross-cutting concerns that apply to business logic operations. They allow you to execute custom code **before** (`preCall`) and **after** (`postCall`) a modeled operation is invoked.

Common use cases include:
- **Auditing**: Creating a log of who performed what action and when.
- **Payload Manipulation**: Modifying input data before an operation or transforming the result after.
- **Side Effects**: Triggering other operations or external service calls in response to an event.
- **Complex Validation**: Enforcing business rules that involve multiple entities or external state.

### OperationCallInterceptor Interface

JUDO provides the `OperationCallInterceptor` interface for intercepting model operations:

```java
public interface OperationCallInterceptor {
    String getName();
    default Collection<EOperation> getOperations(AsmModel asmModel) { return Collections.emptyList(); }
    default boolean async()                { return false; }
    default boolean terminateOnException() { return true; }
    default boolean ignoreDecoratedCall()  { return false; }
    default Object preCall(EOperation operation, Object parameterPayload)
            throws InterceptorCallBusinessException { return parameterPayload; }
    default Object postCall(EOperation operation, Object parameterPayload, Object returnPayload)
            throws InterceptorCallBusinessException { return returnPayload; }
}
```

### Th
[9541] TOOLRESULT: ## Example 6: Handling Both Create and Update

**Pattern**: Single interceptor for create and update with pattern matching

### Create and Update Interceptor Code

```java
@Component(property = { "judo.model.name=<app>" })
public class ChildEntityCreateAndUpdateInterceptor implements OperationCallInterceptor {

    @Reference ParentEntityDao parentEntityDao;
    @Reference ChildEntityDao childEntityDao;
    @Reference ValidateEntity validateEntity;
    @Reference LocationService locationService;
    @Reference DataInterceptorService dataInterceptorService;

    private AsmUtils asmUtils;
    private List<EOperation> interceptedOperations;

    @Override
    public String getName() {
        return this.getClass().getSimpleName();
    }

    @Override
    public Collection<EOperation> getOperations(AsmModel asmModel) {
        if (interceptedOperations == null) {
            if (asmUtils == null) {
                asmUtils = new AsmUtils(asmModel.getResourceSet());
            }

            // Intercept both create and update
            interceptedOperations = Stream.of(
                    "<app>.services.parent_service.ParentEntity#_createInstanceChildEntities",
                    "<app>.services.parent_service.ChildEntity#_updateInstance<app>_services_parent_service_ChildEntity"
            ).map(asmUtils::resolveOperation).map(Optional::orElseThrow).toList();
        }
        return interceptedOperations;
    }

    @Override
    public Object preCall(EOperation operat

[9648] TOOLRESULT: 
For **OR logic**, use the `filterBy(String)` method with a custom JQL (JUDO Query Language) expression:

```java
// OR logic with boolean fields - IMPORTANT: Use == for equality (NOT =)
List<User> users = userDao.query()
    .filterBy("this.isAdmin == true or this.isCoordinator == true")
    .selectList();

// String equality with OR
List<User> users = userDao.query()
    .filterBy("this.userName == 'john.doe' or this.userName == 'jane.doe'")
    .selectList();

// Combining OR with AND in single expression
List<User> users = userDao.query()
    .filterBy("(this.isAdmin == true or this.isCoordinator == true) and this.isActive == true")
    .selectList();

// LIKE pattern - uses method call syntax with ! operator
List<User> users = userDao.query()
    .filterBy("(this.email)!like('%@admin.com') or (this.email)!like('%@company.com')")
    .selectList();

// Complex nested logic
List<User> users = userDao.query()
    .filterBy("(this.isAdmin == true and this.isActive == true) or (this.isCoordinator == true and this.isCanvasser == true)")
    .selectList();
```

**JQL (JUDO Query Language) Expression Syntax:**

| Element | Example | Description |
|---------|---------|-------------|
| Field reference | `this.fieldName` | Access entity field |
| Equality | `==` | Equal comparison (NOT `=`) |
| Not equal | `!=` | Not equal comparison |
| Comparison | `< > <= >=` | Comparison operators |
| Pattern match | `(this.field)!like('%pattern%')` | Method call syntax |
| Boolean | `== true`,

[9666] TOOLRESULT: ## Intent

Part of a page needs UI the generator cannot produce (charts, masked inputs, rich viewers). Model that part as a custom implementation element and supply the React component yourself; the rest of the page stays generated.

## Practice

### Platform base

- A custom implementation is a visual element of the ESM UI model that the generator exposes as an extension point. It is not a way to swap an arbitrary generated component: the element must be modeled first ([widgets: placeholder / custom implementation](../../agent-docs/frontend/esm-to-ui-mappings/widgets.md), [advanced patterns: custom component replacement](../../agent-docs/frontend/advanced-patterns.md)).
- You register a function component as a Pandino service under `CUSTOM_VISUAL_ELEMENT_INTERFACE_KEY` (exported from `~/custom`) with the service property `component` set to the per-element key generated in the container's `customization.ts`. There is no generic `CUSTOM_COMPONENT_HOOK_INTERFACE_KEY`.
- For containers that have such elements the generator creates the scaffold `src/custom/hooks/custom-implementations/register<Container>CustomImplementations.tsx.default` with one `register<Interface>CustomImplementation(context)` function per element and the right props type (`GenericProxyProps`, `TableProxyProps`, `LinkProxyProps` from `~/custom`). Copy it without `.default`, implement the component and call the register function from `application-customizer.tsx` ([hooks: using .default scaffolds](../../agent-do

// The card of exactly this product (descriptions of other products may mention its name).
export const card = (page: Page, product: string) =>
  page.getByTestId('product-card').filter({ has: page.getByTestId('card-name').getByText(product, { exact: true }) });

export async function addFromCard(page: Page, product: string) {
  // wait for the answer of the server: a spec that goes to the cart right away must not leave the add in flight
  await Promise.all([
    page.waitForResponse((r) => r.url().includes('/ShopProductInfo/addToCart'), { timeout: 15000 }).catch(() => undefined),
    card(page, product).getByTestId('add-to-cart').click(),
  ]);
}

// Details page: the generated "Add to cart" action asks for a quantity.
export async function addFromDetails(page: Page, product: string, quantity: number) {
  await apiHelper.triggerAndWaitWithRegex(page, card(page, product), /.*~get$/);
  await page.getByRole('button', { name: /Add to cart/ }).click();
  const d = page.getByRole('dialog');
  await expect(d).toBeVisible();
  const box = d.getByRole('textbox', { name: /Quantity/ }).or(d.getByRole('spinbutton', { name: /Quantity/ }));
  await box.click();
  await box.fill(String(quantity));
  await box.blur();
  await page.waitForTimeout(700);
  await d.getByRole('button', { name: /(Ok|Submit)$/ }).click();
}

dump_postgresql () {
    local INSTANCE_NAME=$1
    local TIMESTAMP=$( date +%Y%m%d_%H%M%S )

    echo "Dumping database..."
    local DUMP_FILE=${schema_name}_dump_$TIMESTAMP.tar.gz
    docker exec -i ${INSTANCE_NAME} /bin/bash -c "PGPASSWORD=${schema_name} pg_dump --username=${schema_name} -F c ${schema_name}" > $DUMP_FILE || exit
    echo "Database dumped to $DUMP_FILE"
}

    mvnd judo-rdbms-schema:apply \
    ${mavenVersionArg} \
    -DjdbcUr
